# B2 Vocabulary

**Level:** Upper Intermediate (B2)
**Total Words:** 1500

This comprehensive vocabulary list contains 1500 upper intermediate-level words across 15 thematic categories. Each entry includes syllable breakdown, IPA pronunciation, part of speech, clear meaning, and a practical example sentence to help you understand usage in context.

---

## Category 1: Academic & Research (100 words)

### 1. academia
- **Syllables:** ac-a-de-mi-a (5 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌækəˈdiːmiə/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** The environment or community concerned with higher education and research
- **Example:** She left the corporate world to pursue a career in academia.

### 2. scholar
- **Syllables:** schol-ar (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈskɒlə/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A specialist in a particular branch of study
- **Example:** He is a renowned scholar in medieval history.

### 3. dissertation
- **Syllables:** dis-ser-ta-tion (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌdɪsəˈteɪʃən/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A lengthy formal treatise on a subject for a doctoral degree
- **Example:** She spent three years working on her dissertation.

### 4. thesis
- **Syllables:** the-sis (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈθiːsɪs/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A statement or theory put forward to be maintained or proved
- **Example:** His thesis challenges conventional wisdom.

### 5. methodology
- **Syllables:** meth-od-ol-o-gy (5 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌmeθəˈdɒlədʒi/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A system of methods used in a particular field
- **Example:** The researcher's methodology was rigorous and well-documented.

### 6. empirical
- **Syllables:** em-pir-i-cal (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ɪmˈpɪrɪkəl/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Based on observation or experience rather than theory
- **Example:** The study provides empirical evidence for the hypothesis.

### 7. quantitative
- **Syllables:** quan-ti-ta-tive (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈkwɒntɪtətɪv/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Relating to measurement of quantity or amount
- **Example:** They conducted quantitative analysis of the data.

### 8. qualitative
- **Syllables:** qual-i-ta-tive (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈkwɒlɪtətɪv/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Relating to quality or kind
- **Example:** The study used qualitative research methods.

### 9. scrutinize
- **Syllables:** scru-ti-nize (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈskruːtənaɪz/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To examine or inspect closely and thoroughly
- **Example:** Experts will scrutinize every detail of the proposal.

### 10. comprehensive
- **Syllables:** com-pre-hen-sive (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌkɒmprɪˈhensɪv/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Including or dealing with all or nearly all elements
- **Example:** The report provides a comprehensive overview.

### 11. rigorous
- **Syllables:** rig-or-ous (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈrɪɡərəs/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Extremely thorough and careful
- **Example:** The selection process is rigorous.

### 12. validate
- **Syllables:** val-i-date (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈvælɪdeɪt/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To demonstrate or support the truth of something
- **Example:** Further research is needed to validate these findings.

### 13. hypothesis
- **Syllables:** hy-poth-e-sis (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /haɪˈpɒθəsɪs/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A proposed explanation made on limited evidence
- **Example:** Scientists test their hypothesis through experiments.

### 14. premise
- **Syllables:** prem-ise (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈpremɪs/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A previous statement from which another is inferred
- **Example:** The argument is based on a false premise.

### 15. inference
- **Syllables:** in-fer-ence (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈɪnfərəns/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A conclusion reached based on evidence and reasoning
- **Example:** What inference can we draw from this data?

### 16. deduce
- **Syllables:** de-duce (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /dɪˈdjuːs/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To arrive at a conclusion by reasoning
- **Example:** From the evidence, we can deduce what happened.

### 17. postulate
- **Syllables:** pos-tu-late (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈpɒstjʊleɪt/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To suggest something as a basis for reasoning
- **Example:** Scientists postulate that life exists on other planets.

### 18. paradigm
- **Syllables:** par-a-digm (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈpærədaɪm/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A typical example or pattern of something
- **Example:** This discovery represents a paradigm shift in physics.

### 19. framework
- **Syllables:** frame-work (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈfreɪmwɜːk/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A basic structure underlying a system or concept
- **Example:** The theoretical framework guides the research.

### 20. criterion
- **Syllables:** cri-te-ri-on (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /kraɪˈtɪəriən/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A principle or standard by which something is judged
- **Example:** What criterion did you use for selection?

### 21. abstract
- **Syllables:** ab-stract (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈæbstrækt/
- **Part of Speech:** noun/adjective
- **Meaning:** A summary of research; existing in thought or as an idea
- **Example:** Read the abstract before the full article.

### 22. synthesize
- **Syllables:** syn-the-size (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪnθəsaɪz/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To combine elements into a connected whole
- **Example:** The paper synthesizes ideas from multiple sources.

### 23. annotate
- **Syllables:** an-no-tate (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈænəteɪt/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To add notes to text giving explanation
- **Example:** Please annotate the manuscript with your comments.

### 24. cite
- **Syllables:** cite (1 syllable)
- **Pronunciation:** /saɪt/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To quote as evidence for an argument
- **Example:** Always cite your sources properly.

### 25. bibliography
- **Syllables:** bib-li-og-ra-phy (5 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌbɪbliˈɒɡrəfi/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A list of works consulted by an author
- **Example:** The bibliography includes 200 references.

### 26. plagiarism
- **Syllables:** pla-gia-rism (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈpleɪdʒərɪzəm/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** The practice of taking someone's work and passing it off as one's own
- **Example:** Plagiarism is a serious academic offense.

### 27. paraphrase
- **Syllables:** par-a-phrase (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈpærəfreɪz/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To express the meaning using different words
- **Example:** Try to paraphrase rather than quote directly.

### 28. critique
- **Syllables:** cri-tique (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /krɪˈtiːk/
- **Part of Speech:** noun/verb
- **Meaning:** A detailed analysis and assessment
- **Example:** The professor offered a constructive critique.

### 29. peer-review
- **Syllables:** peer-re-view (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌpɪəˈrevjuː/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** Evaluation by experts in the same field
- **Example:** The article underwent rigorous peer-review.

### 30. collaborate
- **Syllables:** col-lab-o-rate (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /kəˈlæbəreɪt/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To work jointly on an activity
- **Example:** Researchers from three universities collaborated on this study.

### 31. symposium
- **Syllables:** sym-po-si-um (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /sɪmˈpəʊziəm/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A conference or meeting to discuss a particular subject
- **Example:** She presented her findings at an international symposium.

### 32. seminar
- **Syllables:** sem-i-nar (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈsemɪnɑː/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A small group discussion on a specific topic
- **Example:** The seminar focuses on research methodology.

### 33. lecture
- **Syllables:** lec-ture (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈlektʃə/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** An educational talk to an audience
- **Example:** The lecture covered quantum mechanics.

### 34. curriculum
- **Syllables:** cur-ric-u-lum (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /kəˈrɪkjʊləm/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** The subjects comprising a course of study
- **Example:** The curriculum has been updated.

### 35. pedagogy
- **Syllables:** ped-a-go-gy (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈpedəɡɒdʒi/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** The method and practice of teaching
- **Example:** Modern pedagogy emphasizes active learning.

### 36. assess
- **Syllables:** as-sess (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /əˈses/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To evaluate or estimate the nature or quality
- **Example:** Teachers assess students through various methods.

### 37. cognitive
- **Syllables:** cog-ni-tive (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈkɒɡnətɪv/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Related to mental processes of perception and learning
- **Example:** Cognitive development occurs throughout childhood.

### 38. interdisciplinary
- **Syllables:** in-ter-dis-ci-pli-nar-y (7 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌɪntədɪsɪˈplɪnəri/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Relating to more than one branch of knowledge
- **Example:** The project requires an interdisciplinary approach.

### 39. discipline
- **Syllables:** dis-ci-pline (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈdɪsɪplɪn/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A branch of knowledge typically studied in higher education
- **Example:** Psychology is a diverse discipline.

### 40. theoretical
- **Syllables:** the-o-ret-i-cal (5 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌθɪəˈretɪkəl/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Concerned with theory rather than practical application
- **Example:** The course covers both theoretical and practical aspects.

### 41. practical
- **Syllables:** prac-ti-cal (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈpræktɪkəl/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Relating to the actual doing of something
- **Example:** The program includes practical workshops.

### 42. analytical
- **Syllables:** an-a-lyt-i-cal (5 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌænəˈlɪtɪkəl/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Using logical analysis
- **Example:** Strong analytical skills are essential.

### 43. systematic
- **Syllables:** sys-tem-at-ic (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪstəˈmætɪk/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Done according to a fixed plan or system
- **Example:** They conducted a systematic review of literature.

### 44. coherent
- **Syllables:** co-her-ent (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /kəʊˈhɪərənt/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Logical and consistent
- **Example:** Present a coherent argument.

### 45. ambiguous
- **Syllables:** am-big-u-ous (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /æmˈbɪɡjuəs/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Open to more than one interpretation
- **Example:** The instructions were ambiguous.

### 46. explicit
- **Syllables:** ex-plic-it (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ɪkˈsplɪsɪt/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Stated clearly and in detail
- **Example:** Give explicit instructions.

### 47. implicit
- **Syllables:** im-plic-it (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ɪmˈplɪsɪt/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Implied though not plainly expressed
- **Example:** There's an implicit assumption here.

### 48. objective
- **Syllables:** ob-jec-tive (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /əbˈdʒektɪv/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective/noun
- **Meaning:** Not influenced by personal feelings; a goal
- **Example:** Maintain an objective perspective.

### 49. subjective
- **Syllables:** sub-jec-tive (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /səbˈdʒektɪv/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Based on personal feelings or opinions
- **Example:** Beauty is subjective.

### 50. correlation
- **Syllables:** cor-re-la-tion (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌkɒrəˈleɪʃən/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A mutual relationship between two things
- **Example:** There's a strong correlation between the variables.

### 51. causation
- **Syllables:** cau-sa-tion (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /kɔːˈzeɪʃən/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** The action of causing something
- **Example:** Correlation does not imply causation.

### 52. variable
- **Syllables:** var-i-a-ble (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈveəriəbəl/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** An element that may change
- **Example:** Control for all variables in the experiment.

### 53. constant
- **Syllables:** con-stant (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈkɒnstənt/
- **Part of Speech:** noun/adjective
- **Meaning:** A situation that does not change
- **Example:** Keep the temperature constant.

### 54. deviation
- **Syllables:** de-vi-a-tion (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌdiːviˈeɪʃən/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** The amount by which something differs from a norm
- **Example:** Calculate the standard deviation.

### 55. benchmark
- **Syllables:** bench-mark (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈbentʃmɑːk/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A standard against which things may be compared
- **Example:** This study serves as a benchmark.

### 56. outcome
- **Syllables:** out-come (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈaʊtkʌm/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** The way something turns out; a consequence
- **Example:** The outcome was unexpected.

### 57. implications
- **Syllables:** im-pli-ca-tions (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌɪmplɪˈkeɪʃənz/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** The conclusions that can be drawn from something
- **Example:** What are the implications of this research?

### 58. significance
- **Syllables:** sig-nif-i-cance (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /sɪɡˈnɪfɪkəns/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** The quality of being worthy of attention; importance
- **Example:** The findings have statistical significance.

### 59. validity
- **Syllables:** va-lid-i-ty (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /vəˈlɪdəti/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** The quality of being logically or factually sound
- **Example:** Question the validity of the results.

### 60. reliability
- **Syllables:** re-li-a-bil-i-ty (6 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /rɪˌlaɪəˈbɪləti/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** The quality of being trustworthy or consistent
- **Example:** Test the reliability of the instrument.

### 61. replicate
- **Syllables:** rep-li-cate (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈreplɪkeɪt/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To make an exact copy; to repeat
- **Example:** Other labs tried to replicate the experiment.

### 62. generalize
- **Syllables:** gen-er-al-ize (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒenrəlaɪz/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To make a general statement by inferring from specific cases
- **Example:** Be careful not to over-generalize.

### 63. extrapolate
- **Syllables:** ex-trap-o-late (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ɪkˈstræpəleɪt/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To extend findings to a different situation
- **Example:** We can extrapolate these trends forward.

### 64. interpolate
- **Syllables:** in-ter-po-late (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈtɜːpəleɪt/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To insert something between fixed points
- **Example:** Interpolate the missing data points.

### 65. anomaly
- **Syllables:** a-nom-a-ly (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /əˈnɒməli/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** Something that deviates from what is standard or expected
- **Example:** This result is an anomaly.

### 66. outlier
- **Syllables:** out-li-er (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈaʊtlaɪə/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A data point that differs significantly from other observations
- **Example:** Remove the outliers from the dataset.

### 67. baseline
- **Syllables:** base-line (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈbeɪslaɪn/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A starting point used for comparisons
- **Example:** Establish a baseline before treatment.

### 68. protocol
- **Syllables:** pro-to-col (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈprəʊtəkɒl/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A system of rules or procedures
- **Example:** Follow the experimental protocol carefully.

### 69. parameter
- **Syllables:** pa-ram-e-ter (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /pəˈræmɪtə/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A numerical characteristic of a population
- **Example:** Define the parameters of the study.

### 70. demographic
- **Syllables:** dem-o-graph-ic (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌdeməˈɡræfɪk/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective/noun
- **Meaning:** Relating to the structure of populations
- **Example:** Analyze the demographic data.

### 71. ethnographic
- **Syllables:** eth-no-graph-ic (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌeθnəˈɡræfɪk/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Relating to the scientific description of peoples and cultures
- **Example:** The researcher conducted ethnographic fieldwork.

### 72. longitudinal
- **Syllables:** lon-gi-tu-di-nal (5 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌlɒndʒɪˈtjuːdɪnəl/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** A study that follows subjects over an extended period
- **Example:** This longitudinal study spans 20 years.

### 73. cross-sectional
- **Syllables:** cross-sec-tion-al (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌkrɒsˈsekʃənəl/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** A study analyzing data from a population at one specific point in time
- **Example:** The cross-sectional survey was conducted in 2020.

### 74. retrospective
- **Syllables:** ret-ro-spec-tive (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌretrəˈspektɪv/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Looking back on or dealing with past events
- **Example:** A retrospective analysis of the data.

### 75. prospective
- **Syllables:** pro-spec-tive (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /prəˈspektɪv/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** Expected or expecting to be something in the future
- **Example:** A prospective study will track participants forward.

### 76. control group
- **Syllables:** con-trol group (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /kənˈtrəʊl ɡruːp/
- **Part of Speech:** noun phrase
- **Meaning:** A group in an experiment that does not receive treatment
- **Example:** Compare results with the control group.

### 77. experimental group
- **Syllables:** ex-per-i-men-tal group (6 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ɪkˌsperɪˈmentəl ɡruːp/
- **Part of Speech:** noun phrase
- **Meaning:** The group receiving the treatment in an experiment
- **Example:** The experimental group showed improvement.

### 78. placebo
- **Syllables:** pla-ce-bo (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /pləˈsiːbəʊ/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** A substance with no therapeutic effect used as a control
- **Example:** Half the participants received a placebo.

### 79. double-blind
- **Syllables:** dou-ble-blind (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌdʌbəlˈblaɪnd/
- **Part of Speech:** adjective
- **Meaning:** A study where neither participants nor researchers know who receives treatment
- **Example:** The double-blind trial ensures objectivity.

### 80. randomize
- **Syllables:** ran-dom-ize (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈrændəmaɪz/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To assign subjects to groups by chance
- **Example:** Randomize participants to reduce bias.

### 81. sample size
- **Syllables:** sam-ple size (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈsɑːmpəl saɪz/
- **Part of Speech:** noun phrase
- **Meaning:** The number of observations in a statistical sample
- **Example:** A larger sample size increases reliability.

### 82. population
- **Syllables:** pop-u-la-tion (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌpɒpjʊˈleɪʃən/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** The entire group being studied
- **Example:** The study population included 10,000 adults.

### 83. sampling
- **Syllables:** sam-pling (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈsɑːmplɪŋ/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** The process of selecting a subset of a population
- **Example:** Random sampling ensures representativeness.

### 84. bias
- **Syllables:** bi-as (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈbaɪəs/
- **Part of Speech:** noun
- **Meaning:** Prejudice or systematic error in sampling or testing
- **Example:** Minimize bias in data collection.

### 85. confounding variable
- **Syllables:** con-found-ing var-i-a-ble (7 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /kənˈfaʊndɪŋ ˈveəriəbəl/
- **Part of Speech:** noun phrase
- **Meaning:** An external factor that affects both variables being studied
- **Example:** Age may be a confounding variable.

### 86. operationalize
- **Syllables:** op-er-a-tion-al-ize (6 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌɒpəˈreɪʃənəlaɪz/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To define variables in measurable terms
- **Example:** How do you operationalize "happiness"?

### 87. quantify
- **Syllables:** quan-ti-fy (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈkwɒntɪfaɪ/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To express as a quantity or amount
- **Example:** It's difficult to quantify the impact.

### 88. codify
- **Syllables:** cod-i-fy (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈkəʊdɪfaɪ/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To arrange systematically into a code
- **Example:** Codify the interview responses.

### 89. categorize
- **Syllables:** cat-e-go-rize (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˈkætəɡəraɪz/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To place in a particular class or group
- **Example:** Categorize the data by age group.

### 90. triangulate
- **Syllables:** tri-an-gu-late (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /traɪˈæŋɡjʊleɪt/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To use multiple methods to study the same phenomenon
- **Example:** Triangulate findings using different data sources.

### 91. substantiate
- **Syllables:** sub-stan-ti-ate (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /səbˈstænʃieɪt/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To provide evidence to support or prove the truth
- **Example:** Can you substantiate these claims?

### 92. corroborate
- **Syllables:** cor-rob-o-rate (4 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /kəˈrɒbəreɪt/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To confirm or give support to a statement or theory
- **Example:** Independent studies corroborate the findings.

### 93. refute
- **Syllables:** re-fute (2 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /rɪˈfjuːt/
- **Part of Speech:** verb
- **Meaning:** To prove a statement or theory to be wrong
- **Example:** The evidence refutes that hypothesis.

### 94. contradict
- **Syllables:** con-tra-dict (3 syllables)
- **Pronunciation:** /ˌkɒntrəˈdɪkt/

- **Syllables:** vi-a-ble (3 syllables)

- **Meaning:** Capable of working

## Conclusion

Congratulations! You have completed **1500 upper intermediate (B2 level) vocabulary words** across 15 thematic categories. These words represent the sophisticated language needed for:

- Academic contexts and research
- Professional and corporate environments
- Complex discussions on social, political, and economic issues
- Technical and specialized fields
- Nuanced expression of ideas and emotions

**Next Steps:**
- Review words regularly using spaced repetition
- Practice using new vocabulary in context
- Read authentic B2-level texts (academic articles, quality newspapers, professional reports)
- Write essays and reports incorporating this vocabulary
- Engage in discussions requiring precise, sophisticated language

**B2 Level Achievement:**
At this level, you can:
- Understand the main ideas of complex texts
- Interact with fluency and spontaneity with native speakers
- Produce clear, detailed text on a wide range of subjects
- Explain viewpoints on topical issues with advantages and disadvantages

Continue to the **B2 Grammar Workbook** and **B2 Speaking Practice** files to develop comprehensive upper intermediate proficiency!
